Unexplained Lights

RAL ACC

1. Early in the morning of 27 Bec 89 (approximately O300L3, two USAF
security police patrolmen sa udl Tiyhts outside the back gate at
RAF Woodbridge. Thinking an aircraft might have crashed or been forced

mission to yo outside the gate to investigate.

ta org-
22 Pro

down, they called for pe
The on-duty flight chief responded and allowed three patrols
ceed on foet. The individuals reported seeing a strange glowing object
in the forest. The ebject was described as being metalic in appearance
and triangular in shape, app daimately two to three meters across the
pase and approximately two meters high. Te qlluminated the entire forest
with a white light. The object itself hed a pulsing red light on top and
a bank(s) of blue lights underneath. The object was hovering or on leas.
As the patralmen approached the object, it maneuvered through the trees
and disappeared. At this time the animals on a nearby‘ farm weat into a
frenzy. The object was briefly sighted approximately an hour later near
the back gate. '

é. The next day, three depressions } 1/2" deep and 7?" in diameter were
found where the object had been sighted on the ground. The following
night (29 Dec 80) the area was checked for radiation. Beta/ganma readings
of 0.1 milliroentgens were recorded with peak readings in the three de-
Bressions and near the cencer of the triangle formed by the depressions,

A nearby tree had moderate (.05-.07) readings on the side of the tree
toward the depressions.

3. Later in the night a red sun-like light was seen through the trees.

1. IT attach a draft reply to Merlyn Rees MP. Since Mr Rees is
unlikely to be familiar with the MOD's interest in the subject, the
draft follows the standard line taken with correspondents on UFOs.

ee However, Mr Rees’ constituent, Section40, | represents an

organisation called the Yorkshire JU eoctety (YUFOS) which is well
known to us. The report te which refers was provided by
the MOD in May 1984. [eke] the report, together with our
eovering letter and request, are also enclosed. Clearly
there is nothing we can usefully add to what was said in our letter
Lo Section 40,

3. Also enclosed are copies of 2 PQs which we normally pass to

correspondents who may be interested. One of these, put down by Sir
Patrick Wall on 24 October 1983, specifically mentions the incident
in question.

Hy You may also have seen an article which appeared in The
Guardian on 5 danuary 1985, giving en interesting counter-~argument to
that propounded by YUFCS. A copy of the article is enclosed and US
of SCAF) may like to pass this to Mr Rees for information under

DRAFT
D/US of SCAF)/DGT 48284 February 1985

Thank you for your letter of 4 January, with the enclosed

dé first of all point out that the sole interest of the

joa

io shou

tw

Ministry of Defence in reported sightings of Unidentified Flying
Objects (UFOS) is to establish whether they have any bearing on the

defence of the country.

There is no organisation in the Ministry of Defence appointed
solely for the purpose cf studying UFOs, and no staff are employed on
the subject full time. The reports we receive are referred to the
staff in the Department who are responsible for the air defence of
the United Kingdom, and they examine the reports as part of their

normal duties.

Sinee our interest in UFOs is limited to possible defence
implications we have not carried out a study into the scientific
Significance of these phenomena. Unless there are defence impli-
cations we do net attempt to identify sightings and we cannot inform
observers of the probable identity of the object seen. The
Department could not justify the expenditure of public funds on

investigations which go beyond the pure defence interests.

The only information we have on the alleged "UFO sightings" at
Rendlesham Forest in December 19°80 is the report by Colonel Charles

Hait, of the United States Air Force, of lights seen outside RAF
Woodbridge, a copy of which was sent section 40 | on request and

which he mentions in his letter. We are satisfied that the events

deseribed are of no defence significance.

I am also enclosing with this copies of 2 recent Parliamentary

Questions, one of which mentions the report in question.

Lord Trefgarne

SUBJECT:

DEPARTMENT OF THE AIR FORCE
HEADQUARTERS S151 COMBAT SUPPORT GROUP (USAFE)
APO NEW YORK 07/55

cb 13 Jan 21

Unexplained Lights

RAF /CC

1. Early in the morning of 27 Dec & (approximately O300L), two USAF
security police patrolmen saw unusua}? lights outside the back gate at

RAF Woodbridge. Thinking an aircraft might have crashed or been forced
down, they called for permission to go outside the gate to investigate.
The on-duty flight chief responded and allowed three patrolmen to pro-
ceed on foot. The individuals reported seeing a strange glowing object
in the forest. The object was described as being metalic in appearance
and triangular in shape, approximately two to three meters across the
base and approximately two meters high. It illuminated the entire forest
with a white light. The object itself had a pulsing red light on top and
a bank{s) of blue lights underneath. The object was hovering or on legs.
As the patrolmen approached the object, it maneuvered through the trees
and disappeared. At this time the animals on a nearby farm went into a
frenzy. The object was briefiy sighted approximately an hour later near
the back gate.

2. The next day, three depressions 1 1/2" deep and 7" in diameter were
found where the object had been sighted on the ground. The following
night (29 Dec 80) the area was checked for radiation. Beta/gamma readings
of 0.1 miltiroentgens were recorded with peak readings in the three de-
pressions and near the center of the triangle formed by the depressions.

A nearby tree had moderate (.05-.07) readings on the side of the tree
toward the depressions.

3. Later in the night a red sun-like light was seen through the trees.

It moved about and pulsed. At one point it appeared to throw off glowing
particles and then broke into five separate white objects and then dis-
appeared. Immediately thereafter, three star-like objects were noticed

in the sky, two objects to the north and one to the south, all of which
were about 10° off the horizon. The objects moved rapidly in sharp angular
movements and displayed red, green and blue Vights. The objects to the
north appeared to be elliptical through an 8-12 power lens. They then
turned to full circles. The cbjects to the north remained in the Sky for
an hour or more. The object to the south was visible for two or three
hours and beamed down a streain of light from time to time. Numerous indivi-
duats, including the undersigned, witnessed the activities in paragraphs

2 and 3.

Unidentified Flying Objects

Sir Patrick Wall asked the Secretary of State for
nee (1) how many alleged landings by unidentified
§ objects have been made in 1980, 1981, 1982 and
1935, respectively; and how many have been investigated
by his Department's personnel; -
(2) how many uncaplained sightings there have been jn
F280, 1981, 1982 and 1983, respectively; and which of
these had been traced by radar and with what result.

Mr, Lee [pursuant to his reply; 9 March 1984, ¢. 728):
For the years in question, the Ministry of Defence received
the following numbers of reports of sightings of flying
objects which the observer could not identify: 350, 600,
250, and 390. Reports of alleged landings are not
separately identified. The Department was satisfied that
none of these reports was of any defence significance and,
in such cases, does not maintain records of the extent of
its investigations.
